Updated tasks, created 3 files, loaded tools

Done. Final pin map: data PA0–PA7 (as you wired), RST PB12, CS PB9, DC PB10, WR → PB13, RD → PB14 — move just those two jumpers. That frees PB0 (signal ADC) and PB1 (thermistor ADC), the only ADC channels left on the F411.

Three files in your folder: User_Setup.h (copy into TFT_eSPI), the full instrument_panel.ino, and WIRING.md with the thermistor divider, signal divider + bias + clamp network, and verification checkpoints.

Two things to note:

Update THERM_R25, THERM_BETA, THERM_RSERIES in the sketch with your confirmed values before trusting readings.
The sketch still uses analogRead() (~45 kSPS), so classification is reliable to ~4–5 kHz. Get this working first, then we do the DMA upgrade for the full 11 kHz range.

Also: the KEY button sits on PA0 = LCD_D0 — don't press it while running.

Using library SPI at version 1.1.0 in folder: C:\Users\kavee\AppData\Local\Arduino15\packages\STMicroelectronics\hardware\stm32\2.12.0\libraries\SPI

exit status 1
Compilation error: 'Features' does not name a type
Thought process

That's the classic Arduino .ino preprocessor quirk — it auto-generates function prototypes and inserts them above the struct Features definition, so the compiler sees Features before it's defined. Fix: move the custom types into a header file (includes are processed before the generated prototypes).

Edited 3 files, created a file

Fixed. WaveType and Features now live in analyzer_types.h (same sketch folder), included at the top of the .ino. Since #include lines are processed before Arduino's auto-inserted prototypes, the types are visible everywhere.

Recompile — it should build clean now.

with new pin config, the display is not working, I did not even connected the thermistor or signal or any other pins than pins of the display. It only gives a blinking white display, but for previous pin config, it worked

Thought process

A white screen with new wiring almost always means TFT_eSPI is still driving the old pins — the config mismatch is in the library, not your wires.

Most likely cause: the User_Setup.h I created sits in your project folder, but TFT_eSPI ignores that file. It only reads the one inside the library itself:

C:\Users\kavee\Documents\Arduino\libraries\TFT_eSPI\User_Setup.h

Your compile log confirms it's using that library copy. If that file still holds the old pin map (data on PB0–PB7, or WR/RD on PB0/PB1), the library keeps strobing the old pins while your display is now wired to PA0–PA7 + PB13/PB14 → uninitialized panel → white backlight. The "blinking" is your loop repeatedly hammering a bus the display isn't on.

Fix:

Open C:\Users\kavee\Documents\Arduino\libraries\TFT_eSPI\User_Setup.h
Replace the pin defines with exactly the ones from the User_Setup.h in your project folder (data PA0–PA7, RST PB12, CS PB9, DC PB10, WR PB13, RD PB14, TFT_PARALLEL_8_BIT, ILI9488_DRIVER)
Also open User_Setup_Select.h in the same folder and confirm the line #include <User_Setup.h> is active (not commented out in favor of another setup file)
Rebuild and flash — TFT_eSPI caches nothing, so a clean rebuild picks it up

Let me also give you a minimal display-only test to isolate the panel from the analyzer code:

Created a file

Flash tft_pin_test after updating the library's User_Setup.h. If colors cycle, the pin map is good and the analyzer sketch will work too.

If it's still white after the library file is confirmed correct, check in this order:

Jumpers actually moved: LCD_WR wire now on PB13, LCD_RD on PB14, and nothing left connected to PB0/PB1. A WR wire still on PB1 is the classic miss — WR is the one pin where a mistake gives exactly "white screen, no response."
Data lines: D0→PA0 ... D7→PA7 in order (a swapped pair gives garbage, but all-shifted-by-one gives white/no-init).
Don't press the KEY button during the test — it's hardwired to PA0 = LCD_D0.
Continuity check with your DMM from each shield pin to the Black Pill header pin, with power off.

Report what the test sketch shows and we'll narrow it from there.

Thought process

Found it — the library file still has the old WR/RD pins. You moved the wires to PB13/PB14, but TFT_eSPI is still strobing PB1/PB0. That mismatch is exactly why the panel never initializes.

Change these two lines in that file:

cpp
#define TFT_WR    PB13   // was PB1
#define TFT_RD    PB14   // was PB0

Also add this one line near the top of your active section — your data bus is PA0–PA7, so it enables the ~8x faster port-write path:

cpp
#define STM_PORTA_DATA_BUS

So the block becomes:

cpp
#define STM32
#define TFT_PARALLEL_8_BIT
#define STM_PORTA_DATA_BUS     // data bus on PA0-PA7

#define ILI9488_DRIVER

#define TFT_WIDTH  320
#define TFT_HEIGHT 480

#define TFT_RST   PB12
#define TFT_CS    PB9
#define TFT_DC    PB10
#define TFT_WR    PB13
#define TFT_RD    PB14

#define TFT_D0    PA0
...
#define TFT_D7    PA7

Save, rebuild, flash tft_pin_test — you should get red/green/blue fills. Then the instrument panel sketch will work on the same config.

Now it works but does not show the wavefrom type or frequenct. I only connected PB0 as input to input signal of op amp. It shows a continuous sine wave when sine input is given but also it displays sine type signals when square or ramp waves were given using function generator

Thought process

Two separate problems here.

1. Type/temp text invisible — font bug (my fault). I used Font 6 for the big labels, but Font 6 only contains 1234567890:-.apm — no letters, so "SINE"/"SQUARE" render as nothing. Fixing to Font 4 doubled up:

Edited a file, searched code, read a file

The font fix is already in your file, so the labels should render once the data is sane. The real problem is the input signal itself. Two things:

1. Everything looks sine → your test frequency is too high for analogRead(). Blocking reads cap at ~40–45 kSPS. A square wave is only "square" because of its harmonics (3rd, 5th, 7th...). At a 10 kHz input, the 3rd harmonic is 30 kHz — above Nyquist (~22 kHz) — so it aliases away and all that survives is the fundamental: a perfect sine on your scope, and crest factor ≈ 1.414 → classified SINE. Same for ramp.

Test at 100 Hz – 1 kHz for now. There, a square wave keeps 20+ harmonics and will classify correctly. The full 11 kHz range needs the DMA ADC upgrade (our next step, ~1 MSPS capable).

2. You're feeding the FG straight into PB0 — that's bipolar. The negative half-cycles are being clamped to ~−0.3 V by the pin's internal ESD diode. That distorts the waveform (breaks symmetry/crest factor) and stresses the pin. Until you build the divider + bias network from WIRING.md, configure the function generator itself to output unipolar:

Amplitude: 3 Vpp
DC offset: +1.5 V

→ signal swings 0–3 V, safely inside the ADC range, no clipping.

Then verify with the built-in debug: open Serial Monitor @ 115200. Each line prints fs, vpp, crest, sym, and the classification. With a 500 Hz, 0–3 V input you should see roughly:

Input	crest	sym
Square	~1.0	~0.5
Sine	~1.41	~0.5
Triangle	~1.73	~0.5
Ramp	~1.73	~0.5 (but asymmetric slope)

If crest still reads ~1.41 for a 500 Hz square with proper offset, paste me a few Serial lines and I'll dig further.
